Zimmerman, L. Zimmermann, M. E. Zucker

**Abstract:** We report on a gravitational wave search for compact binary coalescences with secondary mass between 0.2 M and 1 M and primary mass between 0.2 M and 10 M, restricting the component mass ratio to lie between 0.1 and 1. The analysis uses data collected by the LIGO and Virgo detectors between 2024 April 10 15:00:00 and 2025 January 28 17:00:00, the second part of the fourth observing run of the LIGO-Virgo-KAGRA Collaboration. Results from two out of three search pipelines show outlier candidates, but not at a sufficient level of significance to provide evidence for detection. We report the combined sensitivity of the current searches over the first and second parts of the observing run to the target sub-solar mass (SSM) black hole (BH) population, as well as to neutron star (NS) binaries with one or more sub-solar component. We place upper limits on the merger rate of SSM BHs, ranging from $50 {\rm Gpc}^{-3} {\rm yr}^{-1}$ to~$5000 {\rm Gpc}^{-3} {\rm yr}^{-1}$ (90% confidence) for different component mass combinations. We use the merger rate limits to constrain two illustrative dark matter (DM) scenarios that can form SSM compact objects: primordial BHs, and dark BHs forming in a dissipative DM model. Our search limits the fraction of DM in primordial BHs, assuming a monochromatic mass distribution, to be $\leq$ (0.018-0.032) at 1 M, and $\leq$ (0.10-0.18) at 0.35 M for early-forming binaries; or to be less than 1 at masses above (0.62-0.74) M for late-forming binaries. For the dissipative model, we constrain the fraction of DM for a range of minimum dark black hole masses below $1\,M$ to be as low as (1.0-1.1) x $10^{-5}$; we also exclude minimum masses above (0.62-0.74) M, if dark BHs constitute all of the DM. For binary NSs that include sub-solar mass components, assuming a simple, fixed population model we find a merger rate upper limit of $249 {\rm Gpc}^{-3} {\rm yr}^{-1}$.

## 43. [Resonant three-mode coupling in asteroseismology I: Bifurcations, chaos, and DAV white-dwarf outbursts](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.28513) <a id="link43"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.28513

**Authors:** Zhao Guo, Gordon I. Ogilvie

**Abstract:** Resonant three-mode coupling is a fundamental nonlinear process in stellar oscillations and other wave systems. We investigate the global dynamics of a linearly overstable parent mode coupled to two damped daughter modes. The principal control parameters are frequency detuning $\Delta$ and daughter damping rate $\Gamma$, normalized by the parent growth rate. We combine high-resolution maps of the largest Lyapunov exponent in the $(\Gamma,\Delta)$ plane with linear stability analysis, Floquet calculations, and numerical continuation. The resulting parameter space includes stable equilibria, simple and period-doubled limit cycles, chaotic attractors, formally unbounded solutions, and higher-period `shrimp' islands within chaotic regions. Successive period doublings lead to chaos, while boundaries of isolated periodic windows exhibit type-I intermittency.   At large $\Gamma$, the main bifurcation boundaries approach straight lines, indicating that the dynamics is governed primarily by $r=\Delta/(2\Gamma)$. We connect finite-$\Gamma$ bifurcations to an earlier one-dimensional return map. As $\epsilon\rightarrow0$, this map recovers the principal period-doubling sequence, the onset of chaos near $r\simeq0.38$, and the asymptotic positions of prominent periodic windows. We also examine the effects of unequal daughter damping rates.   Applying these results to sporadic outbursts in cool DAV white dwarfs, we find that estimated $g$-mode growth rates, damping rates, and detunings place resonant triplets in both periodic and chaotic regimes. Thus, instability of the three-mode equilibrium need not produce a regular limit cycle: deterministic chaos and intermittency may contribute to irregular outburst amplitudes and recurrence times.

**Novelty:** 6

## 44. [Modular Bayesian Inference for Partially Misspecified Mixtures with Uncertain Class Labels](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.27085) <a id="link44"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.27085

**Authors:** Maximilian Autenrieth, Konstantin Karchev, David A. van Dyk, Kaisey Mandel, Roberto Trotta

**Abstract:** Scientific inference often involves observations from multiple latent populations whose class memberships are uncertain and must be inferred from available data. While full Bayesian mixture modelling provides a principled approach to joint uncertainty quantification in this setting, often the model is misspecified or less trustworthy for populations that are not the target of the inference, which can distort posterior inference for the parameters of interest. We propose a modular Bayesian framework that safeguards against such biases. We decouple classification from downstream inference using fixed class probabilities, for example from a classifier trained on a subset of labelled data. We then protect inference on the parameters of interest by preventing the likelihood contributions of less trustworthy populations from influencing them. A key benefit of this principled modularization is that it greatly simplifies inference, reducing a fully joint mixture analysis to sampling label allocations, fitting the protected downstream model for each sample, and averaging the resulting posteriors. We establish calibration conditions on these class probabilities under which the proposed procedure preserves the inferential target of the analysis with known labels, and demonstrate the method in synthetic experiments and in cosmological analysis of photometric supernova samples.

**Relevance:** 2
**Novelty:** 6

## 45. [The Imprint of Core-collapse Asymmetries on Supernova Shock Breakout](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.27183) <a id="link45"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.27183

**Authors:** David K. Khatami, David Vartanyan, Hai P. Le, Daniel N. Kasen, Adam Burrows

**Abstract:** Shock breakout is the first electromagnetic signal of a supernova, generating a highly luminous and fast-timescale burst of thermal radiation. Motivated by recent theoretical progress in producing successful three-dimensional supernova explosions of massive stars, we undertake a detailed numerical study on the impact that the core-collapse asymmetries might have on the breakout signal. We perform the first fully 3D multi-group radiation hydrodynamics simulations of shock breakout for the case of a red supergiant explosion. We find that large-scale asymmetries formed during the core-collapse process can extend the breakout flash duration to days, with the timescale set by the supernova shock fully wrapping around the progenitor star. Using detailed 3D radiative transfer post-processing, we generate synthetic multi-wavelength light curves and spectra to more closely connect to observations. The light curves show a significant viewing-angle dependence of the overall shape and peak brightness of shock breakout. We find that core-collapse asymmetries have the largest impact on the breakout light curve when observed in shorter UV wavelengths. On the other hand, at optical wavelengths, we find a diminished impact of the asymmetric breakout that more closely resembles a 1D spherical breakout. Our results show how multi-dimensional effects originating from the core-collapse mechanism can play a major role in setting the shock breakout properties, and are particularly relevant to upcoming UV space missions such as ULTRASAT and UVEX.

**Relevance:** 2
**Novelty:** 6

## 46. [Orbital Parameters for 65 Wide Binary Systems: no evidence for gravitational anomaly at low acceleration](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.28220) <a id="link46"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.28220

**Authors:** L. Pasquini, R. Saglia, F. Patat, L. Berni, D. Bossini, L. Magrini, H. -G. Ludwig, M. T. Murphy, J. R. de Medeiros, B. L. Canto Martins, J. Chanamé

**Abstract:** We use a Monte Carlo analysis to study whether wide binaries can be represented by bound Newtonian orbits, and, if this is the case, whether the distribution of the orbital parameters agrees with expectations. The potential orbits are classified according to the $\chi^2$ probability of representing star coordinates, parallaxes, velocity components and masses. We apply this analysis to 44 systems previously published, to which we add 26 wide binaries, consisting of twin low-mass (M) stars. We find orbital solutions for 65 systems, and derive the distribution of their orbital parameters. For one system composed of M-type stars, no Newtonian bound solution can be found; however, this pair is a member of the young Argus association and therefore not a suitable target. The remaining four systems that cannot be fit were already identified as likely disrupted binaries. The uncertainty in the separation between the components, given by the parallax error, is by far the dominant error source, and the main limitation of the present analysis, affecting especially the determination of the orbits' inclination angle and phase. Newtonian dynamics describe well our sample. When dividing the sample into two subsamples, according to the mutual acceleration, the eccentricity and periastron distributions agree with theoretical expectations; inclination and phase are distorted by the parallax-limited separation, more strongly in the high-acceleration (37 systems) than in the low-acceleration ($A_N < 10^{-9} m s^{-2}$, 28 systems) subsample, which is opposite to what a gravitational signal would produce. In particular the ratio between observed velocity difference and circular velocity does not show any excess for the low acceleration sample. When parametrized as a boost $\gamma$ parameter to the velocity, $0.95 < \gamma <1.05 (1 \sigma)$, fully consistent with Newton also for the low acceleration sample.

## 47. [20,000 Rotation Periods in Nearby Open Clusters and Moving Groups from ZTF and ASAS-SN](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.27113) <a id="link47"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.27113

**Authors:** Yuxi Lu

**Abstract:** Open clusters provide uniquely powerful laboratories for studying stellar evolution because their members share a common age and chemical composition. Stellar rotation of low-mass GKM dwarfs in open clusters provides direct empirical constraints on magnetic braking, with implications for stellar interiors, magnetic activity, and star--planet interactions. However, existing cluster rotation samples are dominated by space-based observations, whose limited spatial coverage and short observing baselines can make it difficult to construct an unbiased sample. Long-baseline ground-based time-domain surveys therefore provide an important complement to space-based photometry. I present a catalog of $\sim$20,000 periodic photometric signals, including stellar rotations, eclipsing-binaries, and classical pulsators, measured from the Zwicky Transient Facility (ZTF) and the All-Sky Automated Survey for Supernovae (ASAS-SN) for members of nearby open clusters and moving groups within 1.5 kpc. This catalog provides a new empirical foundation for studying the mass, chemical composition, and age dependence of stellar angular-momentum evolution and for testing models of magnetic braking across a broad range of stellar masses and evolutionary states.

**Relevance:** 2
**Novelty:** 6

## 48. [A Natural Dark Matter-Dark Energy Interaction from Axion-Monopole Dynamics](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.27059) <a id="link48"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.27059

**Authors:** Hengameh Bagherian, Matthew Reece, Lian-Tao Wang, Huangyu Xiao

**Abstract:** We propose a natural framework for interacting dark matter and dark energy based on axion-monopole dynamics in a dark gauge sector. Dark matter is composed of dark 't Hooft-Polyakov monopoles, while dark energy is an ultralight axion whose coupling to the dark gauge field induces an axion-dependent dyon mass through the Witten effect. This generates a direct dark matter-dark energy interaction. We show that generic models with a significant dark matter-dark energy coupling suffer from a naturalness problem: the Coleman-Weinberg potential induced by the interaction is typically much larger than the dark energy scale. In our model, the corrections arise from a monodromy tower of dyon states, allowing them to be exponentially suppressed. In the minimal setup, the interaction strength is predicted to be $\sim 0.1\%$, below the sensitivity of current observations. We discuss scenarios in which cosmological population of higher-charge dyons can enhance the interaction to the percent level, producing an apparent phantom dark energy equation of state. We further show that the parameter space of the axion-monopole model preferred by fitting the Dark Energy Spectroscopic Instrument (DESI) results can also naturally explain the cosmic birefringence with a small anomaly coefficient.

**Relevance:** 2
**Novelty:** 5

## 49. [J-PLUS: Identifying Outlier Spectral Energy Distributions in the Fourth Data Release Using Unsupervised Machine Learning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.27252) <a id="link49"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.27252

**Authors:** F. Quispe-Huaynasi, F. Roig, V. Loaiza-Tacuri, V. M. Placco, V. Marra, F. Jiménez-Esteban, A. del Pino, B. Anguiano, J. A. Alzate-Trujillo, S. Daflon, C. López-Sanjuan, P. Cruz, A. Alvarez-Candal, E. Machado-Pereira, J. Alcaniz, R. E. Angulo, J. Cenarro, D. Cristóbal-Hornillos, R. Dupke, A. Ederoclite, A. Hernán-Caballero, C. Hernández-Monteagudo, A. Marin-Franch, M. Moles, L. Sodré Jr, J. Varela, H. Vázquez-Ramió, J. Zaragoza-Cardiel

**Abstract:** The selection of candidates belonging to rare or underrepresented stellar populations, as well as novel objects, is essential for spectroscopic follow-up. In this work, we demonstrate how combining broad- and narrow-band photometric information with machine-learning techniques enables this selection. Using photometric data in twelve filters from the Javalambre Photometric Local Universe Survey (J-PLUS) as input, we applied an anomaly detection technique based on autoencoders to identify spectral energy distributions (SEDs) that deviate from the dominant pattern in the J-PLUS objects sample. Subsequently, we applied the t-distributed stochastic neighbor embedding (t-SNE) algorithm to the J-PLUS colors of the outlier SEDs population. Combining information from the SIMBAD database and other astronomical surveys to classify the outliers identified by the autoencoder, we are able to characterize over-densities in the t-SNE map. Our study specifically focuses on well-defined islands in the t-SNE space, corresponding to specific populations. We identified a large sample of white dwarfs and low-mass stars, including a small subset of polluted white dwarfs, and report potential candidates for spectroscopic follow-up, including 19 carbon-rich stars, 11 cataclysmic variables and 170 white-dwarfs-main-sequence binaries, all previously unreported in the literature.

## 50. [The Future of Gamma-Ray Bursts as Probes of the Epoch of Reionization in the Era of JWST and the Extremely Large Telescope](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.27282) <a id="link50"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.27282

**Authors:** H. M. Fausey, A. J. van der Horst, N. R. Tanvir, K. Wiersema

**Abstract:** The Epoch of Reionization (EoR) is a key era of cosmological history that remains poorly understood. An examination of the Ly-alpha damping wing of high-redshift GRBs can provide direct measurements of the fraction of neutral hydrogen (i.e., neutral fraction) of the IGM in the vicinity of the GRB host galaxy, in addition to the neutral column in the host along the sight-line to the GRB. The latter quantity can help infer the fraction of ionizing photons escaping the galaxy (i.e. escape fraction); the average escape fraction being a key input to EoR models. New and upcoming observatories such as the JWST and the Extremely Large Telescope (ELT) can obtain more robust estimates of the hydrogen column density and neutral fraction around high-redshift GRBs. We simulate spectra for the JWST NIRSpec and ELT HARMONI spectrographs using different GRB magnitudes and exposure times to determine their capabilities for measuring column densities and neutral fractions with the Ly-alpha damping wings of high-redshift GRBs. We find that both spectrographs will be highly effective for obtaining column density estimates. Despite the presence of telluric lines, we find that ELT HARMONI will be a powerful instrument for retrieving accurate neutral fraction estimates, but JWST NIRSpec will be vital for obtaining neutral fraction measurements for bright GRBs that fall in redshift ranges where ELT is less effective. Additionally, shorter JWST Target of Opportunity (ToO) follow-up times would greatly increase the accuracy of neutral fraction estimates obtained with NIRSpec spectra.

**Novelty:** 5

## 51. [On the superbursts as photodisintegrators of ashes from the rp-process -- Numerical simulations of superbursts with MESA II](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.28049) <a id="link51"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.28049

**Authors:** M. Nava-Callejas, S. Goriely, N. Chamel

**Abstract:** Context: Superbursts are energetic explosions in the crusts of neutron stars in Low-Mass X-ray Binaries (LMXBs). At temperatures above 4 GK, photodisintegration of heavy rp-process ashes may play a major role alongside $^{12}$C fusion. Aims: We investigate how photodisintegration of rp-process ashes affects superburst properties and the initial $^{12}$C abundance required for ignition. Methods: We simulate superbursts with MESA v.24.08.1. Besides the canonical fuel of $20\%$ $^{12}$C and $80\%$ $^{56}$Fe, we test different $^{12}$C abundances mixed with scaled rp-process ashes. Results: Superbursts can ignite with only $10\%$ $^{12}$C when the remaining $90\%$ consists of rp-process ashes. Their luminosity is comparable to that of the canonical fuel, whereas $10\%$ $^{12}$C mixed with $90\%$ $^{56}$Fe yields lower luminosity. $^{56}$Fe remains the most abundant ash nuclide. Although photodisintegration drives nucleosynthesis, neutron, proton and alpha captures release energy at late times. We also find that $A=64$ nuclides or $^{20}$Ne help reduce the $^{12}$C abundance needed for ignition. Conclusions: The composition of accreted material - not just its $^{12}$C content - substantially affects superburst ignition, luminosity and nucleosynthesis.

**Novelty:** 5

## 52. [Probing Nearby Electron Sources and Local Diffusion with the TeV All-Electron Spectrum](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.27635) <a id="link52"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.27635

**Authors:** Zhen Xie, Ruizhi Yang

**Abstract:** Radiative losses restrict multi-TeV cosmic-ray electrons to young nearby accelerators, allowing the all-electron spectrum (electrons plus positrons) to retain information about particle release and local transport. We investigate how a nearby source can shape this spectrum using Vela as a representative example. We compare a birth-time burst with continuous release for the same total injected electron energy and examine the dependence on the diffusion coefficient, injection index, and source energetics. The burst spectrum develops a cooling-limited boundary near 28 TeV at the age of Vela, whereas continuous release produces a broader high-energy tail through the contribution of recently injected particles. The flux at Earth is largest over a finite range of diffusion coefficients, reflecting the competition between inefficient arrival and spatial dilution. Harder injection spectra and larger released energies increase the source contribution in the multi-TeV region, and we map the combinations of injection index and released electron energy that can produce an observable spectral feature. For anisotropic diffusion, the source visibility also depends on the angle between the Vela-Earth direction and the local ordered magnetic field. Future direct measurements extending the all-electron spectrum beyond several TeV could therefore distinguish different release histories and constrain both the energetics of nearby sources and local cosmic-ray transport.

**Relevance:** 2
**Novelty:** 5

## 53. [A Solar Optical Clock](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.28485) <a id="link53"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.28485

**Authors:** Connor Fredrick, Ryan K. Cole, Ryan Terrien, Suvrath Mahadevan, Scott A. Diddams

**Abstract:** Precision spectroscopy underpins quantum sensing, atomic timekeeping, and the search for terrestrial-mass exoplanets. Finding an Earth-Sun analog requires fractional splitting of spectral features that rivals the precision of the best laboratory spectroscopy. This corresponds to resolution of a few parts in $10^5$ of a typical stellar spectral line, or a Doppler velocity precision of less than 10 cm s$^{-1}$. Only a few astrophysical spectrometers reach this regime, and only by dispersing starlight with large gratings and averaging thousands of lines. Here, we show that comparable precision can be achieved on a single solar line by leveraging techniques used in quantum sensors and optical clocks. A laser is frequency-locked to the 1565 nm solar Fe I line by coupling to a single mode of the solar thermal field and counted against an optical frequency comb referenced to the SI second, thus creating a solar optical clock. Operating at the fundamental photon-noise limit of thermal heterodyne spectroscopy, we achieve centroid precision of one part in $10^9$ of the optical frequency, or 30 cm s$^{-1}$ in radial velocity. We recover the Sun's five-minute acoustic oscillations and the expected Rossiter-McLaughlin signal during a solar eclipse. Combining these techniques from quantum sensing with a thirty-meter-class telescope could enable our demonstrated precision on the nearest Sun-like stars in a few hours. Multiplexing such absolute frequency measurements across lines with different magnetic sensitivity and formation depth would reduce averaging time and help disentangle stellar granulation and activity from the Doppler signature of terrestrial exoplanets, providing new approaches to address some of the most demanding measurements in astrophysical spectroscopy.

**Novelty:** 7

## 54. [Identifying a jet-shaped point-symmetric morphology in the supernova remnant HB9](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.28428) <a id="link54"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.28428

**Authors:** Noam Soker (Technion, Israel)

**Abstract:** I identify a point-symmetric morphology in the supernova remnant HB9 (G160.9+2.6), supposedly a core-collapse supernova (CCSN) remnant (CCSNR), and attribute this structure to two or more pairs of jets that exploded the star within the jittering-jets explosion mechanism (JJEM). The pairs of jets that shape the point-symmetric morphology of HB9 are part of a larger number of pairs that exploded the star. The point-symmetric structure is composed of a pair of ears, a pair of ears/nozzles, a pair of dents, and a pair of bright bars. Although these pairs of morphological features are small and the point-symmetric structure is subtle, I consider the identification robust. I use visual inspection to identify the point-symmetric morphology of HB9, a method commonly used to identify jet-shaped, point-symmetric morphologies in other astrophysical objects, such as cooling-flow clusters of galaxies and planetary nebulae. I present arguments for jet-driven shaping rather than CSM shaping. These findings support earlier claims that the JJEM is the primary explosion mechanism of CCSNe.

## 55. [Axion detection in the non-Markovian noise](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.27731) <a id="link55"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.27731

**Authors:** Jing Dong, Shuang-Quan Ma, Rong-Hang Chen, Bin-Kai Luo, Tao Liu, Qing Ai, Jun-Hong An

**Abstract:** The detection of axion dark matter poses a significant challenge due to its exceptionally weak coupling to standard-model particles. Here we develop a noise-tolerant quantum-metrology scheme based on coupled $^{129}$Xe spin ensembles to search for such weakly interacting particles. Within an experimentally accessible parameter regime, our scheme achieves an ideal exclusion limit on the axion coupling constant that is several orders of magnitude more stringent than that of conventional detection methods. We further investigate the performance of our scheme under dissipative noise and find that its robustness is fundamentally governed by the energy-spectrum structure of the composite spin-environment system. Remarkably, when a bound state emerges in the spectrum, the exclusion limit asymptotically recovers its ideal value in the non-Markovian dynamics, whereas under the Markovian approximation the limit deteriorates significantly due to noise. Our results establish a universal mechanism for noise-resilient exploration of beyond-standard-model physics, which can be practically implemented by engineering bound-state formation via tuning the Larmor frequency of the spin ensembles.

## 56. [A Multipole Technique for the Simulation and Analysis of Gravitational Waveforms from Extreme Mass Ratio Inspirals](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.27153) <a id="link56"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.27153

**Authors:** Courtney McIntosh (Howard University, Multimessenger Astrophysics Branch GSFC), Jeremy Schnittman (Multimessenger Astrophysics Branch GSFC)

**Abstract:** We introduce EMRICode, a Python-based numerical framework for evolving generic extreme mass-ratio inspirals (EMRIs) in Kerr spacetime. The code combines adaptive numerical integration of test-particle geodesics with a prescribed model for the secular evolution of the orbital constants of motion, allowing eccentric and inclined trajectories to be followed over long inspiral timescales. We focus in particular on the treatment of the Carter constant, whose evolution is not directly determined by the leading-order fluxes for energy and axial angular momentum used in the present model. We investigate several prescriptions for determining its evolution and find that a radial-potential-based prescription provides the most stable behavior among those considered, while also exhibiting a systematic residual bias for nearly planar orbits. We compare the resulting inspirals with established radiation-reaction approximations, including the weak-field treatment of Peters and Kerr-based prescriptions from the literature, and use these comparisons to assess the qualitative behavior and limitations of the implementation. The modular structure of EMRICode allows the radiation-reaction prescription to be replaced independently of the underlying geodesic evolution, providing a framework for incorporating more accurate flux models and additional physical effects. We present two simple examples of how interactions with accretion disks and partial tidal disruptions can drive the evolution of a stellar EMRI orbit. EMRICode therefore provides a flexible computational framework for studying the orbital dynamics of EMRIs and for exploring extensions beyond the idealized vacuum, point-particle approximation.

## 57. [Constraining the FRB Central Engine from Flux Variations of Persistent Radio Sources: FRB 20240114A as the First Attempt](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.27489) <a id="link57"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.27489

**Authors:** Hirotaka Matsumiya, Kazumi Kashiyama, Tomonori Totani

**Abstract:** Some repeating fast radio bursts (FRBs) are associated with persistent radio sources (PRSs), likely driven by the long-term activity of a central engine. We investigate FRB 20240114A-currently the most active repeating FRB-to explore the link between its burst activity and the flux variability of its associated PRS. We model electrons responsible for FRB emission being continuously injected from a central magnetar into its magnetar wind nebula (MWN), where synchrotron radiation produces the PRS. Comparing our model with the latest PRS spectra and flux variations, we constrain the physical properties of the FRB source. We find that the total energy injected into the MWN during FAST observations from January to August 2024 is $\sim 10^{48} \, \rm{erg}$, consistent with the magnetic energy budget of a magnetar with an internal magnetic field of $\sim 10^{16} \, \rm G$. The bulk Lorentz factor of the FRB-emitting plasma is mildly constrained to $\lesssim 10^3$, and FRB 20240114A is inferred to be powered by a young magnetar aged $\sim 10$-$20 \, \rm {yr}$. These results show that the PRS flux variability effectively probes FRB emission properties. We predict that while PRS luminosity increases rapidly with enhanced FRB activity, it will slowly fade over decades via adiabatic cooling after FRB activity ceases. Energetics suggest that the hyper-active phase observed in FRB 20240114A (burst rate $\sim 700 \, \rm hr^{-1}$) is a rare state with a duty cycle of $\lesssim 10^{-2}$, which is broadly consistent with its detection rate in current FRB-PRS samples.

## 58. [Dynamical stability of Magnetic Supramassive Neutron Stars](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.28609) <a id="link58"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.28609

**Authors:** Jorge L. Rodríguez-Monteverde, Sebastiano Bernuzzi, William Cook, Maximilian Jacobi, Brynmor Haskell

**Abstract:** We investigate the dynamical stability of strongly magnetized supramassive neutron stars close to the mass-shedding limit using fully general-relativistic magnetohydrodynamic simulations in three spatial dimensions. Equilibrium configurations are constructed using a microphysical equation of state and considering poloidal magnetic fields up to maximum strengths ${\sim} 10^{17}\,\mathrm{G}$. The initially unstable magnetic fields reorganize into mixed poloidal--toroidal configurations on Alfv\'en timescales shorter than the stellar rotation period. The subsequent dynamical evolution is governed by timescales set by the Lehnert number. For stars with masses sufficiently lower than the maximum mass, magnetic stresses redistribute angular momentum and drive the development of differential rotation, leading to a new quasi-stationary configuration that remains dynamically stable over several rotation periods. Magnetically-driven winds develop from the outer layers around the equatorial plane and remain baryon-matter dominated with magnetization ${\lesssim}0.2$. Over the simulated timescale, winds carry a total rest mass of order ${\sim}10^{-3}\mathrm{M}_\odot$ at outflow rates of up to a few $M_\odot/\mathrm{s}$. For stars close to (or beyond) the maximum-mass turning point along the stellar equilibrium sequence, strong magnetic fields trigger gravitational collapse by redistributing angular momentum away from the stellar core. The resulting black hole remnants are characterized by angular velocities up to ${\sim}49\%$ larger and angular momentum ${\lesssim}8\%$ smaller than those of the initial equilibrium stars. Our results show that extreme magnetic-field instabilities can significantly alter the structure of supramassive neutron stars, with possible implications for magnetars and long-lived binary neutron-star merger remnants.

Nührenbörger, B. Nichol, W. Noga, B. Owens, R. Ørsøe, L. Papp, N. Park, V. Parrish, P. Pfahler, J. Pflanz, B. Pirenne, E. Price, A. Rahlin, M. Rangen, E. Resconi, N. Retza, S. Robertson, M. F. **Abstract:** Neutrino telescopes instrument optically transparent media with three-dimensional arrays of photosensors to detect Cherenkov radiation induced by neutrino interactions within or near the instrumented volume. The small cross-sections of the weak interaction and the low neutrino fluxes above TeV energies require vast detector volumes, which only natural media on Earth can realistically provide. Spurred by the detection of the first neutrino sources by the IceCube Neutrino Observatory, a new generation of neutrino telescopes is emerging to increase the global instrumented volume and detection sensitivity. The Pacific Ocean Neutrino Experiment (P-ONE) aims to install such a telescope in the Northeast Pacific Ocean, building on established neutrino telescope design targets in collaboration with Ocean Networks Canada (ONC). This article presents the design, integration, and testing of the first P-ONE line, P-ONE-1, in preparation for its deployment and integration into ONC's deep-sea observatory NEPTUNE. Alongside the detector design and integration methods, we detail in-air and in-water system-level validation performed at several test sites in Canada, culminating in the successful construction of P-ONE-1 and its preparation for deployment.

## 60. [A sub-Earth-sized TESS planet candidate in the nearby M-dwarf binary GJ 774, most likely orbiting GJ 774 A (TIC 351339274)](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.28575) <a id="link60"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.28575

**Authors:** Jaden Tripp

**Abstract:** Small planets transiting the nearest M dwarfs are the terrestrial planets most accessible to detailed characterisation. We report a sub-Earth-sized transiting planet candidate in the wide (17.6") M-dwarf binary GJ 774 at 12.86 pc, most likely orbiting the M2.5 primary GJ 774 A (TIC 351339274). The signal was a SPOC multi-sector threshold-crossing event (MES = 7.95) and is not a TESS Object of Interest. In eight TESS sectors (2019-2026) a 1.28-h transit recurs every 4.225138 +/- 0.000010 d with a box-mean depth of 365 +/- 32 ppm in A's light. The 2026 data, which entered neither the SPOC search nor our selection, show it at the ephemeris predicted from 2019-2023 data (304 +/- 43 ppm; none of 10^5 phase-scrambled versions of the 2026 data is as deep). The per-target false-alarm probability is < 0.03 at 95%. Odd/even, secondary-eclipse, archival-plate and Gaia radial-velocity tests exclude unrelated blended eclipsing binaries and eclipsing companions on 4.2-d orbits, and hierarchical eclipsing binaries are improbable. The stars are 0.85 TESS pixel apart, so the TRICERATOPS false-positive probability (0.26) is set by a planet on GJ 774 B. B is disfavoured because its own light does not dim enough: PRF-deblended photometry gives 109 +/- 115 ppm in B's light, against 1089 ppm required if B were the host. Conditional on a point source at A or at B, the joint host model gives P(A)=0.59-0.97 (0.97 for the native target-pixel baseline). Over all sectors the PRF photometry is consistent with A, but in the out-of-sample 2026 data 40% of the deficit appears at B's position, an unexplained 2-3 sigma (PSF-model and depth dependent) tension with host A. On A the planet has R_p=0.70 +/- 0.06 R_Earth, S=15.0 +/- 1.8 S_Earth and T_eq=548 +/- 16 K. It is not validated; resolved ground-based photometry of the pair would test host B directly.

## 61. [Adaptive Model-Switch Thermodynamic Integration: A Bayes Factor Estimator](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.24354) <a id="link61"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.24354

**Authors:** Amanda Malnati, Megan Kralj, Ken D. Olum

**Abstract:** Bayes factors are a widely used model comparison criterion, but are often quite challenging and computationally expensive to estimate well. We propose a variation of the model-switch thermodynamic integration method for estimating Bayes factors that decreases the variance of the estimator and essentially eliminates integration error. We choose samples whose density in $\beta$ is proportional to the standard deviation of previously drawn samples. Rather than averaging many samples at each $\beta$, we use a very dense set of $\beta$ values, collecting one sample at each in order of increasing $\beta$ and one sample at each in order of decreasing $\beta$ to minimize "thermic lag". We study our procedure with a toy model (based loosely on some signal recovery problems). In this model we find that adaptive sampling gives us a 2.7-fold decrease in the spread of the estimated Bayes factor. We test our procedure both drawing directly from the posterior distribution and using a Markov Chain Monte Carlo sampler.

## 62. [Slow-Roll Inflation at Arbitrary Order](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.28608) <a id="link62"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.28608

**Authors:** William Giarè

**Abstract:** We derive explicit expressions for the primordial scalar and tensor spectra at arbitrary finite order $p$ in the slow-roll expansion, valid both for canonical single-field inflation and $k$-inflation. At any order, the spectra are determined by known combinations of slow-roll parameters weighted by universal coefficients entirely fixed by the free de Sitter solutions, without requiring perturbatively corrected mode functions. Using these spectra, we obtain closed-form consistency relations that connect the tensor tilt $n_t$ and its runnings $\alpha_n^t\equiv d^n n_t/d(\ln k)^n$ to the tensor-to-scalar ratio $r$, the scalar spectral index $n_s$, and its runnings $\alpha_n^s\equiv d^n(n_s-1)/d(\ln k)^n$. The consistency relations are valid for arbitrary $n\geq 0$ and any finite $p\geq 0$, without recursion over lower-order relations. For $k$-inflation, they additionally depend on the scalar sound speed and its scale derivatives. We develop an algorithm that generates both the spectra and consistency relations for any pair $(n,p)$. We reproduce known results through next-to-next-to-next-to-leading order (N3LO) and extend the explicit calculations up to N10LO.

## 63. [Exponential Growth of Turbulent Cross Helicity in Shear-Flow-Driven Turbulence](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.28424) <a id="link63"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.28424

**Authors:** B. Tripathi, A. E. Fraser, P. W. Terry, E. G. Zweibel, M. J. Pueschel, A. M. Beloborodov

**Abstract:** Cross helicity, the velocity--magnetic-field correlation, is an ideal invariant of incompressible magnetohydrodynamics and constrains energy cascades and magnetic-field generation. Although cross helicity typically decays via visco-resistive effects, we analytically demonstrate that it grows exponentially in sheared inhomogeneous turbulence. This cross-helical shear instability is confirmed using three-dimensional numerical simulations. Growth requires a nonzero gradient of the Reynolds--Maxwell stress to produce turbulent cross helicity; this production overcomes effective losses when shear exceeds a threshold set by the ratio of turbulent magnetic to momentum diffusivities.

**Abstract:** Wide-field cosmic microwave background (CMB) instruments have reached sufficient sensitivity and cadence to conduct untargeted millimeter-wave searches for astronomical transients. To date, such searches have primarily discovered stellar flares, while extragalactic detections remain limited to a few flaring blazars and active galactic nuclei. In contrast, recent simulations suggest that current and future CMB experiments should detect a few to dozens of extragalactic transients per year from sources including gamma-ray bursts (GRBs), tidal disruption events, and fast blue optical transients. At timescales under one week, predicted detections are dominated by flares from reverse shocks in long-duration GRBs, with some benchmark models predicting rates of ~10 detections per year for the SPT-3G camera on the South Pole Telescope. In this work, we conduct a transient search optimized for the bright and brief millimeter-wave emission from GRB reverse shocks. We update previous simulations by incorporating a more realistic model of the SPT-3G sensitivity, observing cadence, and detection algorithm, resulting in nearly a factor of two improvement in detection sensitivity compared to prior estimates. We subsequently carry out a search over four seasons of the 1500 deg2 SPT-3G Main field observations from 2019-22, finding no candidate extragalactic events. We use the lack of detections to constrain the parameters of the synthesized GRB populations, setting upper limits on the fraction of GRBs with reverse shock components, f_RS, and the all-sky rate of reverse shocks beamed at Earth, N_all-sky. For a standard 10^51 erg GRB with Lorentz factor Gamma = 100, we constrain f_RS <0.57 and N_all-sky <1226 bursts/year (at 90% confidence). The most readily detectable model we consider is a 10^52 erg "dirty" GRB with Gamma = 50, whose population we constrain to f_RS <0.09 and N_all-sky <120 bursts/year.

## 65. [Deglobalising the fit: how to search for physics beyond vacuum General Relativity in LISA catalogues](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.27069) <a id="link65"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.27069

**Authors:** Francisco Duque, Jonathan Gair

**Abstract:** The Laser Interferometer Space Antenna will observe up to $\sim 10^{7}$ overlapping signals, which have to be characterised simultaneously with a global fit. This is a challenge for model comparison since rerunning the global fit for every physical model of interest, including deviations from vacuum General Relativity, is computationally demanding. An alternative is a two-step post-processing strategy: a first global fit with vacuum models only, after which a selected group of target sources is reanalysed under a different model with all other sources subtracted. We show that in this strategy a beyond-vacuum effect in a target source can be partly absorbed by the overlapping nuisance sources in the first fit, subtracted with them, and never reach the reanalysis. In the linear-signal approximation, the loss is captured by a single number, the masking ratio $\mathcal{R}\in[0,1]$. A deviation the global fit would detect at $n_\sigma$ is reported by post-processing at $\mathcal{R}\,n_\sigma$, and bounds in null tests are also reported too tight by the same factor. We show how to compute $\mathcal{R}$ from the posterior samples of the vacuum global fit, and give an algorithm that ranks the nuisance sources by how much of the effect each can absorb, so that only those most correlated with it need to be refitted with the target in a down-scaled reanalysis with a few sources. We validate our method with full Markov-chain Monte Carlo inference on a toy model of 21 overlapping chirping sinusoids, where one carries a small non-linear chirp that represents the missed physics, while the other 20 act as nuisance sources. This method can serve as a basis for tests of General Relativity in realistic LISA mock datasets and can be adapted to study the impact of systematics between different gravitational-wave sources.

## 66. [Source velocities of fast radio bursts constrained by their dispersion measure variations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.27311) <a id="link66"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.27311

**Authors:** S. K. Ocker, A. L. Piro

**Abstract:** Growing evidence suggests that many fast radio bursts (FRBs) originate from extragalactic magnetars. Like all neutron stars, magnetars may be kicked at birth to large velocities, the values of which directly inform the physics governing stellar explosions and compact object formation. A subset of repeating FRBs show significant variability in their dispersion measures (i.e., electron column densities) over time, often exhibiting apparently secular trends. Here we show that all repeating FRBs with significant dispersion measure variations can be generically explained by motion of the source. We use existing measurements to constrain the minimum space velocities of the FRB sources, which are found to be $\gtrsim 20-2800\,{\rm km\,s}^{-1}$. We simultaneously constrain the plasma properties of the circumsource media, which are comparable to those of compact HII regions. For sources with compact radio nebulae, we directly infer the maximum source ages, and find them to be consistent with the ages of known Galactic magnetars. Our findings suggest that significant DM variability is a hallmark of young sources born with natal kicks through prompt formation channels. Applying our methods to a larger sample with continued monitoring of repeating FRB dispersion measures will reveal key physics of compact object formation.

## 67. [Gaussian process-based Bayesian imaging of bolometric (sub-)millimetre data](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.27733) <a id="link67"></a>

**Abstract:** Widefield single-dish telescopes operating at (sub-)mm wavelengths ($\lambda \sim 4$~mm to 350~$\mu$m) tend to feature much faster mapping speeds than radio interferometers at the same wavelengths, and are more suitable for measuring signals on larger spatial scales. However, the data are dominated by atmospheric fluctuations on large scales and by photon and instrumental noise on smaller scales, making it difficult to recover the true astronomical sky. We build on maria-nifty, a Gaussian process-based Bayesian imaging framework for reconstructing sky maps from single-dish telescope data, implemented on top of Numerical Information Field Theory (NIFTy). It uses modular generative models to efficiently separate the astronomical signal from atmospheric emission while providing pixel-wise uncertainty quantification for the reconstructed sky maps. Moreover, our method cleanly separates individual astrophysical signals, even though they are superposed on the sky. Following its successful validation on synthetic time-ordered data generated using the maria software, we apply it to real data observed with the MUSTANG-2 bolometric array on the 100-meter Green Bank Telescope. For the galaxy cluster RXJ1347.5-1145, we separate the extended cluster emission from the compact source at its centre and obtain a smoother map with fewer artefacts compared to a standard maximum-likelihood reconstruction using the same data. The JAX-based implementation and GPU acceleration provide the basis for applying the method to the vastly larger datasets that the next generation of single-dish telescopes will deliver.

## 68. [Direct Urca process facilitated by non-Fermi-liquid quantum criticality](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.28023) <a id="link68"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.28023

**Authors:** Hao-Fu Zhu, Guo-Zhu Liu

**Abstract:** Conventional Fermi-liquid theories of dense nuclear matter predict that the direct Urca process requires a proton fraction above a threshold of $Y_p \approx 0.11$--$0.15$, which is rarely reached in low-mass neutron stars. Here we show that this stringent kinematic constraint is drastically relaxed by the non-Fermi liquid behavior arising from superfluid and superconducting quantum criticality in neutron stars. In the non-Fermi-liquid regimes, nucleons have a smooth momentum occupation without a sharp Fermi surface, and their chemical potentials develop a logarithmic temperature dependence. These intrinsic features fundamentally alter the standard $\beta$-equilibrium relation. Consequently, direct Urca neutrino emission can be activated even at low nuclear densities. This modifies the long-standing Urca-threshold paradigm and indicates that direct Urca process has the opportunity to occur in low-mass neutron stars.

**Authors:** Paul D. Lasky, Alexander Clevinger, Andre G. da Silva, Ricardo L. S. Farias, Veronica Dexheimer

**Abstract:** Gravitational-wave observations of neutron star mergers allows us to probe the dense portion of the nuclear equation of state. We develop a formalism for testing the existence and order of phase transitions in the cores of neutron stars using only gravitational-wave observations of their inspiral. We test our formalism on data from the first binary neutron star mergers GW170817 and GW190425, and use synthetic injections assuming the next and uber-next upgrades of the LIGO observatories, A+ and A#, as well as with Cosmic Explorer. We show that populations of mergers in either A+ or A# will be able to measure the existence of a first-order phase transition in observations lasting less than one PhD (less than half a US PhD!). On the other hand, with only the inspiral, we cannot distinguish populations of neutron stars with no phase transition from those with second- or third-order phase transitions or with first-order phase transitions with relatively large transition densities.

**Abstract:** We first derived the formulae of recursive reduction and dlog-form differential equations for arbitrary tree-level cosmological correlator, taking the external relations of massless bulk propagators into consideration. The differential equations could be systematically solved and provide the efficient evaluation of them. We have implemented this methodology as a user-friendly Mathematica package MadStree for computing arbitrary tree-level cosmological correlators.

## 71. [Illuminating Light Dark Matter with Fixed-Target Experiments](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.27131) <a id="link71"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.27131

**Authors:** Taylor R. Gray

**Abstract:** Astronomical and cosmological observations provide overwhelming evidence for a non-luminous matter component, dark matter (DM), whose particle nature remains unknown. Experimental searches for non-gravitational DM interactions have largely targeted DM with GeV-TeV masses via nuclear recoils, yielding no discovery thus far. DM in the MeV-GeV mass range, however, is theoretically motivated, reproducing the observed relic abundance while remaining invisible to nuclear recoil searches. Such light DM is better probed via electron recoils or at fixed-target experiments. This thesis advances the theoretical and statistical toolkit for light DM searches at fixed-target experiments, spanning model building, statistical inference, global fits, and signal simulation. We survey the landscape of fixed-target searches for light DM, focusing on the Light Dark Matter eXperiment (LDMX). We also simulate DM signatures at the next-generation experiment SHiP to assess its sensitivity. Within minimal DM models, we present a likelihood-based statistical framework for LDMX using the full two-dimensional recoil electron distribution, spanning exclusion, discovery, parameter estimation, and model comparison in frequentist and Bayesian formulations. This establishes the discovery potential of LDMX, and we quantify the direct detection exposure needed to independently confirm a potential signal. Beyond this paradigm, we perform global fits of scalar and fermionic light DM, combining cosmological, astrophysical, and laboratory constraints. We then extend fixed-target searches to spin-1 DM and higher electromagnetic moment dark photons, yielding rich signatures with strong complementarity to other probes. Finally, we go beyond standard nuclear modelling of dark photon production, incorporating elastic nuclear form factors and spectral functions from many-body ab initio methods in place of analytic treatments.

## 72. [Detection and Statistics of large-scale Sector Boundary Crossings in the Solar Wind: The SBCross Catalog](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.28139) <a id="link72"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.28139

**Authors:** Stephan G. Heinemann, Manuela Temmer, Mathew Owens, Emilia Kilpua, Charles Farrugia, Daniel Price

**Abstract:** Heliospheric sector boundary crossings (SBCs), composed of the Heliospheric Current Sheet (HCS), Heliospheric Plasma Sheet (HPS), and surrounding plasma halo, represent fundamental structures in the solar wind that influence interplanetary dynamics and space weather. We present the \textsc{SBCross} catalog, an automated, publicly available database containing 1,076 SBC events derived from in-situ observations spanning more than 30 years (1995--2026), covering Solar Cycles 23 and 24 alongside the rising and maximum phases of Solar Cycle 25. By combining interplanetary magnetic field (IMF) and 255~eV suprathermal electron pitch angle distributions (PAD) data, our algorithm reliably captures large-scale polarity transitions, with $76\%$ of detected SBCs exhibiting clear HPS signatures ($\beta > 1$, $n_{\mathrm{peak}} > 10\mathrm{\,cm^{-3}}$). Across the catalog, we find an average occurrence rate of $\approx 2.6$ events per Carrington rotation, a median event duration of $\approx 9.0$\,hours, and a median physical thickness of $\approx 7.4 \times 10^6$\,km. Furthermore, approximately $\approx 15\%$ of detected SBCs interact with interplanetary coronal mass ejections (CMEs) on average, with this proportion rising up to $33\%$ during solar maximum. These CME-associated events exhibit systematically longer durations (median of $10.0$\,hours), $54\%$ larger physical thicknesses, and higher average solar wind velocities. Notably, all high-velocity SBC events ($> 600\mathrm{\,km\,s^{-1}}$) are exclusively driven by CME interactions, demonstrating local plasma pile-up and structural entanglement resulting from fast CMEs overtaking the ambient slow background. The \textsc{SBCross} catalog serves as an updated successor to the classic SBC list by L. Svalgaard, delivering a standardized, open-access resource for the heliophysics community to systematically investigate sector boundary transitions.

## 73. [Localizing binary neutron stars with the Einstein Telescope: a full Bayesian comparison of the triangular and 2L configurations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.27081) <a id="link73"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.27081

**Authors:** Filippo Santoliquido, Jacopo Tissino, Marica Branchesi, Ulyana Dupletsa

**Abstract:** The Einstein Telescope (ET), the European next-generation gravitational-wave observatory, is at a decisive stage: the choice of its detector configuration is still open, with profound implications for its scientific output. We provide a quantitative basis for this choice by comparing two candidate configurations at their proposed sites: the triangular design ($\Delta$) at the Euregio Meuse-Rhine site, and the 2L design, with interferometers in Sardinia, Italy, and Lusatia, Germany. We present the first realistic estimates of sky-localization uncertainties for binary neutron star (BNS) mergers, moving beyond Fisher-matrix forecasts to full Bayesian parameter estimation that captures the multimodal structure of the sky posterior. We focus mainly on the high-frequency interferometers alone, providing an expected scenario for the first years of ET operations, as the more challenging low-frequency instruments will come online later. BNS injections span different sky positions and luminosity distances between 40 and 2000 Mpc, corresponding to signal-to-noise ratios (SNRs) from 12 to 720. Our analysis relaxes the long-wavelength approximation and includes Earth's rotation and higher-order modes, all of which improve sky localization. We find that the $\Delta$ configuration localizes low- and moderate-SNR sources in eight degenerate sky modes spread across both hemispheres and over the day and night sides of the Earth, with 90% credible areas reaching $\sim 2 \times 10^4 \, \mathrm{deg}^2$. In contrast, the 2L configuration recovers a single dominant mode that contains at least 50% of the posterior probability and is one to two orders of magnitude smaller in area. This represents a substantial advantage for multimessenger follow-up campaigns.

## 74. [Cosmic birefringence from a stiff axion field and its cosmological implications](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.27082) <a id="link74"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.27082

**Authors:** Andrea Minotti, Yunzhi Wu, Tiziano Zanzarella, Nicolao Fornengo, Marco Regis, Marco Taoso

**Abstract:** We propose a model in which the observed cosmic microwave background birefringence arises from the interaction of photons with a stiff ($w=1$) pseudo-scalar field that today contributes a subdominant fraction of the total energy density, distinct from both dark matter and dark energy. Big Bang Nucleosynthesis constrains the cosmological density of the stiff field, leading to a lower bound for its coupling with photons, $g_{\phi\gamma} > 8.9 \times 10^{-14}\,\mathrm{GeV}^{-1}$. Photon conversion in cluster-hosted quasars sets an upper bound, $g_{\phi\gamma} < 5.0 \times 10^{-13}\,\mathrm{GeV}^{-1}$. Therefore, there is a narrow window for the discovery of this cosmological component that can be explored in the forthcoming years with astrophysical axion searches. Moreover, we show that this scenario leads to gravitational-wave signals that can be tested with planned experiments.

## 75. [An improved self-gravity treatment in general relativistic smoothed particle hydrodynamics](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.27775) <a id="link75"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.27775

**Authors:** Pritam Banerjee, Kowsona Chakraborty, Niles Mondal, Tapobrata Sarkar

**Abstract:** General relativistic smoothed particle hydrodynamics (GRSPH) on a fixed black hole (BH) spacetime is suitable for fluid bodies that are much less massive than the BH, but the self-gravity of the fluid is added as an extra term in the equation of motion. Standard GRSPH implementations add this as a Newtonian source term to the momentum equation, which is no longer valid when the fluid moves at a sizeable fraction of the speed of light, close to the BH. We develop an improved prescription by deriving the weak gravitational field of each fluid element in its local rest frame and transforming it to the global frame where the momentum equation is solved. The resulting force uses rest-frame distances and softening lengths, accounts for the time dilation of the receiving particle, and its motion relative to the source particle. The derivation applies to any weakly self-gravitating flow on a fixed BH background, provided the body is small compared with its distance from the BH. Implemented in a tree-based GRSPH code, the prescription reproduces the self-gravity of a boosted polytrope with a median error below 1\% up to a Lorentz factor of 2, and keeps a binary of two polytropes stable over ten orbits. As an application, we study tidal disruption events (TDEs) of solar-type stars by supermassive BHs. The treatment of self-gravity shifts the disruption threshold and changes both the remnant core mass and the debris energy distribution. Newtonian self-gravity leaves substantial surviving cores in encounters that our prescription predicts to be nearly complete disruptions.

## 77. [A Radiation-Hardened, Fault-Aware Quantized Neural Network That Self-Heals](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.27621) <a id="link77"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.27621

**Authors:** Peter Xiangyuan Ma, Joshua S. Bloom

**Abstract:** Deep learning (DL) models are increasingly being deployed into radiation-challenging environments in spacecraft, particle accelerators, and, soon, space-based data centers. Instantiated as a learned orchestration of numbers stored in persistent memory, models are susceptible to continual degradation as high-energy particles corrupt data. At inference time, new data passed through the network fundamentally break the off-line assumptions and guarantees of a static model determined during training. To preserve the quality and efficacy of the models, fault tolerance against bit flips is a clear objective. In this work we developed a runtime method for repairing the quantized weights of neural networks (NNs) after bit flips on commodity, commercial off-the-shelf (COTS) hardware, without storing extra replicas. Our work extends the well-established Fault-Aware Training (FAT) paradigm by adding an alignment penalty to concentrate undamaged activations and weights into a low-rank subspace, and we then reconstruct flagged corrupted coordinates from the uncorrupted survivors. We call this method {\it Projection Healing} and apply it to six regression benchmarks on quantized NNs. Holding a FAT checkpoint fixed and toggling only the runtime heal gives a further ${\sim}23\%$ reduction in the native-unit excess integral. Across those datasets the method extends the operational scrub interval by a median of $2.04\times$ at a $+5\%$ RMSE degradation and $1.96\times$ at $+50\%$, compared with $1.62\times$ and $1.07\times$ for classical Single Error Correction, Double Error Detection (SECDED). This introduces a new path towards designing resilient data-driven models, complementing established hardware redundancy, ECC, and FAT-based techniques.

## 78. [Numerical shock broadening and supernova neutrino conversion: implications for DUNE](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.27158) <a id="link78"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.27158

**Authors:** Rafael C. R. de Lima, Pedro Martins Bailer

**Abstract:** Hydrodynamical simulations spread the supernova shock front over a few grid zones, a width set by the numerical scheme rather than by the physics of the shock. Where the front crosses the H resonance, that width can make the computed MSW evolution artificially adiabatic. We quantify the effect on six three-dimensional blue-supergiant explosion models from the Garching archive by propagating individual rays twice: through the stored profile, and with the front replaced by a narrow reconstruction, 2.2 km wide, that approximates a sudden crossing while keeping the densities on either side. The stored fronts are 1.6 to 1.7 grid zones wide. Across twelve configurations, each chosen inside the energy band in which that model's front meets the resonance, the reconstruction raises the ray-averaged crossing probability $P_H$ by 0.44 to 0.76 in absolute terms, median 0.56; narrowing the reconstruction thirty-fold changes each difference by at most 0.055. Along all 12878 rays of one configuration, the sudden-limit formula evaluated on each ray's own densities agrees closely with the propagated reconstructed result. The change is largest when the density jump spans the resonance, over a band of energies that can be estimated from the presupernova density once the shock radius and the compression are known, with tails outside it. In a separate calculation with a parametrised shock and a published emission model, time-dependent conversion changes the $\nu_e$ charged-current count in a 40 kt argon detector by -4.4%, with a sign set by the flavour composition of the source. Folding the reconstructed three-dimensional fronts with a consistent emission model remains to be done.

## 79. [Optical and Ultraviolet Emission from the Gas Photoionized by Gaia BH3: Archival Constraints and Predictions for the 2029 Periastron](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.27075) <a id="link79"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.27075

**Authors:** Nico Cappelluti, Fabio Pacucci

**Abstract:** Gaia BH3, a $32.7\,M_\odot$ black hole (BH) on an 11.6-yr, $e=0.73$ orbit around a metal-poor giant at $\approx590$ pc, accretes from its wind. Neither its mass-loss rate $\dot M_{\rm w}$ nor the fraction of captured gas reaching the BH is known. We ask whether optical and ultraviolet lines from gas photoionized by the hot flow are observable and what they reveal. Unlike X-ray and radio data, they count the ionizing photons the gas absorbs and trace its density and velocity. With Cloudy we compute the H$\alpha$ and He II 4686 emission of the wind and inflow along the orbit for $\dot M_{\rm w}=10^{-11}$-$10^{-8}\,M_\odot\,{\rm yr^{-1}}$, Eddington ratios $f_{\rm Edd}=10^{-8}$-$10^{-5}$ and wind speeds of 30 (slow) and 100 km s$^{-1}$ (fast), and test each model against archival UVES and ESPRESSO spectra. $f_{\rm Edd}$ mainly sets the lines; in dense models they come from gas falling freely onto the BH, spread over thousands of km s$^{-1}$. The archival spectra exclude $\dot M_{\rm w}>2\times10^{-9}\,M_\odot\,{\rm yr^{-1}}$ at $f_{\rm Edd}=2\times10^{-6}$ and $>2.6\times10^{-10}\,M_\odot\,{\rm yr^{-1}}$ at $10^{-5}$ (95%, slow wind), Eddington ratios already excluded by the apastron Chandra limit, $f_{\rm Edd}<7.6\times10^{-7}$. Of two simulated campaigns, only 25 epochs around the 2029 periastron go beyond existing limits. For a slow wind, whose capture rate is constant, a $\ge5\sigma$ H$\alpha$ detection would imply an accretion state 2-3 times above the 2024 limit. For a fast wind, whose captured fraction rises 14-fold toward periastron, the campaign detects $\dot M_{\rm w}\simeq10^{-8}\,M_\odot\,{\rm yr^{-1}}$ for $f_{\rm Edd}\gtrsim1.2\times10^{-6}$, which the apastron limit allows. Below the Chandra limit, a slow wind produces no detectable line.

## 80. [Ion Heating in Pair-loaded Relativistic Turbulence](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26731) <a id="link80"></a>

**Authors:** Yusen Wang, Zhonghai Zhao, Xinyu Zhu, Xianxu Jin, Zhipeng Zhang, Bin Qiao

**Abstract:** Pair-loaded relativistic turbulence is expected in compact high-energy astrophysical environments and may sustain plasma heating. Using self-consistent three-dimensional radiative particle-in-cell simulations, we identify the mechanism of ion heating mediated by pair-enhanced radiative feedback in relativistic turbulence. We derive the theoretical criterion for the transition to a regime dominated by radiative feedback and show that pair loading promotes the growth and persistence of large, connected optically thick structures. These long-lived structures sustain intense localized ion heating, repeatedly generating prominent hot-ion clumps and substantially enhancing the mean ion temperature. Our results establish a physical link between pair creation and sustained ion heating, providing a theoretical basis for investigating intermittent hot-ion production in black-hole coronae and gamma-ray burst outflows.

## 81. [Two-Component Dark Matter and Strongly First-Order Electroweak Phase Transition via a Two-Step Mechanism in the Inert Doublet plus Real Singlet Model](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.28014) <a id="link81"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.28014

**Authors:** Deeksha Dattaram Naik, Arnab Chaudhuri

**Abstract:** We study a two-component dark matter model in which a real singlet scalar and an inert doublet scalar are simultaneously present alongside the Standard Model, each stabilized by an independent discrete symmetry. The real singlet undergoes a strong first-order transition at high temperature, establishing an intermediate phase with a non-zero singlet vacuum expectation value, which in turn generates the barrier required for the Higgs field to complete a strongly first-order electroweak transition at a lower temperature. This two-step mechanism naturally enforces the condition $v_c/T_c \geq 1$, the sphaleron washout-avoidance criterion necessary for electroweak baryogenesis. The gravitational wave spectrum from both steps is computed; the second step produces a decihertz signal, peaking near $0.08$--$0.34~\mathrm{Hz}$ across the viable parameter space and $0.14$--$0.22~\mathrm{Hz}$ at the benchmark points, that reaches the sensitivity of DECIGO for the strongest transitions and of BBO more widely, while the first remains sub-threshold for all planned detectors. At the coupling values required to drive the phase transition, the singlet is generically underabundant as a thermal relic, so the inert doublet carries the dominant fraction of the observed relic density. Confronting the model with current constraints, we find that the LUX-ZEPLIN 2024 result excludes the entire electroweak-phase-transition-viable parameter space of the singlet sector across the full mass range, even after rescaling by the singlet relic fraction, establishing a complete no-go for the singlet as a viable thermal dark matter candidate in this framework.

## 82. [Gamma-ray Bursts as Cosmological Probes Calibrated with Strongly Lensed Quasars and Neural Networks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.27009) <a id="link82"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.27009

**Authors:** Wei Hong, Luca Izzo, Massimo Della Valle, Orlando Luongo, Marco Muccino, Tong-Jie Zhang

**Abstract:** Gamma-ray bursts (GRBs) extend distance measurements to redshifts beyond those routinely probed by Type Ia supernovae, but their use in cosmology is limited by calibration circularity and possible redshift-dependent effects. We investigate strongly lensed quasars as external calibrators of the Amati and Combo GRB relations. We reconstruct the luminosity-distance relation using an artificial neural network trained on TDCOSMO strong-lensing distances, with the absolute scale inherited from the lensing-only calibration. To avoid extrapolation, only GRBs within the lens-supported redshift interval are used for calibration. We then infer cosmological parameters through a hierarchical Bayesian analysis of flat $\Lambda$CDM, with $w_0w_a$CDM examined as a consistency test. For flat $\Lambda$CDM, Amati yields $H_0=78.26^{+17.87}_{-16.30}\,\mathrm{km\,s^{-1}\,Mpc^{-1}}$ and $\Omega_m=0.670^{+0.223}_{-0.276}$, while Combo yields $H_0=78.71^{+13.83}_{-11.29}\,\mathrm{km\,s^{-1}\,Mpc^{-1}}$ and $\Omega_m=0.635^{+0.235}_{-0.242}$. Amati is relatively insensitive to the phenomenological redshift term, whereas Combo shows a stronger dependence. Injection--recovery tests indicate that an unmodelled positive Combo trend can bias $H_0$ and $\Omega_m$ upward. Strong-lens calibration therefore offers a route to extending the GRB Hubble diagram, but the inferred $H_0$ is not an independent GRB measurement: it retains the TDCOSMO distance scale. The precision is limited by GRB scatter, redshift-dependent systematics, and lens-modelling uncertainties.

## 83. [High-cadence GW astrometry in the $\mu$Hz gap](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.27221) <a id="link83"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.27221

**Authors:** Giorgio Mentasti, Carlo Contaldi

**Abstract:** We forecast the sensitivity of a dedicated, fast-spinning astrometric survey to a stochastic gravitational-wave background (SGWB) in the microhertz gap between pulsar timing arrays (PTAs) and LISA. Spinning the satellite faster samples each star more often, raising the Nyquist frequency and opening higher frequencies at no cost in noise: the shorter, noisier exposures are exactly compensated by their number, so the white-noise floor of each position stream is independent of cadence. High frequencies are where astrometry is most competitive, because the deflection measures the strain directly: its strain noise is white, whereas the equivalent noise of pulsar timing grows as $f^2$. With a cross-correlation estimator over $N$ reference stars and the astrometric overlap computed in the distant-source limit, we find that a Gaia-like satellite scanning the ecliptic poles, with $N\sim10^4$ bright stars over $10$~yr, falls short of the background measured by PTAs by a factor $\sim\!60$. A larger, $8$-m-class aperture, or a constellation of satellites covering the whole sky, reaches it, while extending the reach continuously across the $\mu$Hz band.

**Authors:** Yian Yu, Yang Chen, Lulu Zhao, Kathryn Whitman, Ward Manchester, Tamas Gombosi

**Abstract:** Solar energetic particle (SEP) events, operationally defined as proton flux exceeding 10 pfu in the >10 MeV channel, threaten spacecraft systems, astronaut safety, and high latitude aviation, yet continuous real-time forecasting with adequate lead time remains difficult. We present SEPNET-PRISM-R, a near-real-time system that issues hourly, 24 hour ahead probabilistic SEP forecasts. The model integrates multimodal solar data - including active region patches, flare features, coronal mass ejection (CME) characteristics, and historical proton flux - to concurrently predict SEP occurrences, peak proton flux, soft X-ray flux, and CME velocity, with the primary objective being SEP forecasting. Building on existing literature, SEPNET-PRISM-R enhances operational performance by incorporating a postprocessing module that enables cross-validated decision making and interpretable quantification of feature importance. The training dataset spans from February 1986 to September 2025, with subsequent forecasts generated sequentially through the present via a real-time space weather prediction platform that provides predictive probabilities, feature attributions, and historical analogs, enabling more reliable and informed operational decision-making. The proposed framework substantially suppresses false alarm rates while maintaining robust event detection capabilities, as evidenced by case studies and aggregate statistics.

## 85. [Coevolution of Dust Growth and Gravitational Instability in Massive Protostellar Disks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.28331) <a id="link85"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.28331

**Authors:** Ryota Yamamuro, Ryoki Matsukoba, Kei E. I. Tanaka, Eduard I. Vorobyov

**Abstract:** Massive protostellar disks are expected to sustain high accretion rates through gravitational instability, while dust growth may modify their opacity, cooling efficiency, and stability. Conversely, the time-dependent and nonaxisymmetric structures produced by gravitational instability may regulate the local conditions for dust growth. However, the coupled evolution of dust growth and gravitational instability in massive protostellar disks has not yet been explored self-consistently. We present two-dimensional hydrodynamic simulations that follow gas dynamics, dust growth, and opacity-dependent radiative cooling in a massive protostellar disk. We find that dust grains rapidly grow to millimeter-centimeter sizes shortly after disk formation, substantially reducing the infrared opacity. The enhanced cooling promotes disk fragmentation and produces more frequent accretion-driven luminosity outbursts than in a model without dust growth, with durations and peak luminosities comparable to those observed in massive protostars. Dust growth is broadly regulated by the fragmentation-limited size in dense disk regions, whereas newly formed outer disk regions and low-density regions deviate from this limit because of finite collision timescales and dust transport. These results suggest that dust growth may facilitate episodic accretion and fragmentation, while gravitationally unstable disk structures in turn regulate the dust growth process.

**Novelty:** 5

**Abstract:** The discovery of exoplanets around white dwarfs uniquely informs our understanding of planetary survival through stellar evolution. Common Envelope (CE) evolution is one of the proposed mechanisms that could shape the orbital architecture of these planets around stellar remnants. In this work, we predict the occurrence rate and orbital separation/planet mass distributions for planets that could form around white dwarfs through CE evolution. We simulate populations of giant-planet systems, sampling initial conditions from observed mass and semi-major axis distributions for main sequence stars. We evolve the host star from main sequence to white dwarf to assess which planetary configurations could survive, and we predict the resulting post-CE planet population. Our results suggest that such planets should be rare, massive, and have very close orbital separations. We find that most of the surviving planets have masses of $>10\,M_{\rm{Jup}}$ and orbital separations $2a_{disrupt}$. The properties of CE survivors from our simulations contrast with the known closely-orbiting planet WD 1856+534 b, suggesting a different mechanism may be responsible for its formation.

## 87. [Exomoonlight Sonata: On the Fate of Exomoons Around Migrating White Dwarf Planets](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.27484) <a id="link87"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.27484

**Authors:** Ritvik Sai Narayan, Joseph R. Livesey, Juliette Becker

**Abstract:** The survival of exomoons around close-in white dwarf planets offers a potential constraint on their migration histories and an alternative pathway to post-main-sequence habitability. We investigate whether moons can survive the tidal circularization of planets via high-eccentricity migration, and consider a hierarchical three-body system (white dwarf-planet-moon) after an initial eccentricity excitation of the planetary orbit. Using an analytic equilibrium tidal model, we compare the evolution of the planetary and satellite orbits and examine the conditions required for an evection resonance. We show that the moons of pseudosynchronously rotating planets always migrate inwards since the planetary corotation radius always exceeds the Hill radius, independent of mass and orbital eccentricities. Comparing the migration timescales for the moon and the planet shows that compact moons undergo rapid tidal decay relative to the outer orbit. However, the wider separations favored by slower migration timescales experience pericenter perturbations whose work constitutes a substantial fraction of the moon's orbital energy. Together, our results indicate that moons likely do not survive during a planet's high-eccentricity migration phase in a three-body configuration.

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.27121

**Authors:** B. Vaia, F. Gastaldello, S. Molendi, I. Bartalucci, M. Balboni, S. Ghizzardi, L. Lovisari, M. Marelli, M. Rossetti, A. Tiengo, C. Macculi, S. Lotti, V. Fioretti, T. Mineo, G. Schellenberger, C. E. Grant

**Abstract:** We present a study of the unfocused particle-induced background measured by the XMM-Newton EPIC-MOS2 detector, extending previous analyses to the end of 2025 and covering approximately twenty-five years of observations. Using out-of-field-of-view (outFOV) data, we investigate the long-term evolution of the particle background over two complete solar cycles and compare it with independent tracers of the Galactic cosmic-rays. We find an extremely tight correlation between the MOS2 outFOV count rate and the AMS proton flux, with an intrinsic scatter of only about 1.1\%, confirming that variations in the unfocused background are primarily driven by changes in the Galactic cosmic-rays flux. The extended temporal baseline clearly reveals the modulation associated with the solar cycle and the characteristic signatures of the 22 year Hale cycle. A Lomb-Scargle analysis identifies a dominant periodicity at 11.28 yr, while structure-function and wavelet analyses reveal a persistent $\sim$27 day modulation associated with solar rotation. We also confirm the presence of a residual background component that does not scale with the Galactic cosmic-ray flux, consistent with previous studies and possibly associated with Compton-scattered high-energy X-ray photons. This work places the particle-induced background measured by the XMM-Newton EPIC-MOS2 detector in the broader context of Galactic cosmic-ray studies, while also providing direct implications for the development of background-monitoring and mitigation strategies for future X-ray missions such as \textit{NewAthena}.

## 89. [Automated optimization of solar energetic particle acceleration and transport parameters against five-spacecraft observations of the 9 October 2021 event](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.28603) <a id="link89"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.28603

**Authors:** Hiroyuki Tako Ishikawa, Naho Fujita, Yuta Kato, Miki Kurihara, Takashi Minoshima, Daikou Shiota, Kazumasa Iwai, Marco Pinto, António Pessanha Gomes, Go Murakami, Kanya Kusano, Chihiro Mitsuda

**Abstract:** Solar energetic particle (SEP) events threaten space infrastructure, yet physics-based prediction models depend on numerous poorly constrained free parameters. We introduce the Fujitsu Solar Energetic particle Intelligent Tuning and Evaluation Network (SEITEN), which couples the SUSANOO MHD model of CME propagation to SEPIF, a diffusive shock acceleration and focused transport model, through the shock conditions SUSANOO supplies, and automatically optimizes the free parameters of the particle model against the SEP event of 9 October 2021. The mean free path and the injection efficiency vary independently along each magnetic field line and heliolongitudinal bin, giving a 19-dimensional problem solved by quasi-Monte Carlo sampling followed by a Tree-structured Parzen Estimator. The optimized model reproduces proton flux profiles at five spacecraft with a mean absolute error of $\approx 0.45$ in $\log_{10}$ flux, and the inferred mean free paths show a clear east-west structure; these are effective parameters conditional on the assumed transport geometry. Six independent runs show that whether mean free paths or injection efficiencies dominate depends on the MHD shock strength, and that injection efficiencies differ by orders of magnitude between MHD configurations despite nearly identical fit quality. Joint MHD-SEP parameter estimation is therefore a necessary next step towards generalized, operational SEP forecasting.
